# BnF italien 1584 f. 64 -- Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, Milan 6 Mar 1447 (cipher slip): reading

Siamo stati avisati da Fosceo et da Contuzo, et rechesti che gli voglamo dare favore, per dubio che hano de le gente
de li inimici, le quale sono adunate et pasate de za da Oglio in Cremonese, et intendano andare ad Cremona per [t]orre
quella terra, et questo medesemo havemo sentito da molti et diversi altri lochi, per la quale cosa havemo subito
mandato in quelle parte Mesere Carlo, Mesere Alberto di Pii, el Conte Carlo con le gente loro; havemoli etiam
mandato Mesere Biaxo de Azareto con l'armata per aqua, et havemo comiso ad Petro Vesconte chi è ad Piziguitone, et ad
Zorzo de Anone el quale è ad nome nostro con l[e] dicte [gente d'arme], che ad ogni rechesta de queli vostri vadano
in lor aiuto et favore, et per salvarve quela terra fazano tuto quelo et come sarà bisogno, et come fariano per la
persona nostra propria, et così ad ogni nostra posanza s'inzegnaremo et perforzaremo de dargli ogni subsidio et
socorso, perché ve la posano salvare, ad che ve ne havemo voluto avisare, perché sapiati el tuto in che termini stano
li facti vostri de Cremona. Avisandove che se ne fusemo stati avisati ad bona hora haveresemo ben proveduto per modo
che non solamente non se sariano metuti ad questo, ma non pur ad pensarlo. Data Mediolani die sexto Marcii.
[Dux Mediolani] et cetera.

(Square brackets: [t] = a letter the slip miswrites, [e] = a letter the slip omits; [gente d'arme] = the name sign ÷9,
[Dux Mediolani] = looped δ∞.)
Translation

f64_en.md

# BnF italien 1584 f. 64 -- Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, Milan 6 Mar 1447 (cipher slip): English

The Duke has been told by Fosceo and Contuzo, who ask for his help, that the enemy's troops (the Venetians') have
gathered and crossed the Oglio into the Cremonese and mean to march on Cremona to take the city; many other places
report the same. He has at once sent Messer Carlo (Gonzaga), Messer Alberto Pio and Count Carlo with their men, and
Messer Biagio d'Assereto with the fleet by water, and has ordered Pietro Visconti at Pizzighettone and Giorgio
d'Annone, who holds the troops in his name, to go to the aid of Sforza's people at their first request and do all
they would do for the Duke himself to save Cremona; he will spare no effort to help. Had he been warned in time, he
would have arranged things so that the enemy would not even have thought of it.

f. 65, Milan, 6 Mar 1447, duplicate of the same day

The duplicate of f. 64, sent the same day.

Transcription

f65.md

# BnF italien 1584 f. 65 -- Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, duplicate (6 Mar 1447) of the letter of 6 Mar 1447 (cipher slip)

Canvas 62, lower slip (about x 4020-6620, y 2620-4240 of `img/c62.jpg`). Key R5323 (`key.md`) with the slip shapes of
ff. 30, 36, 37 (`texts/key_additions_B.md`). Read sign by sign from 2x line strips (three overlapping pieces per line)
and 2.6-4x zooms of `img/c62.jpg`; checked against the later clear text on f. 63 (`texts/clear_ff29_35_63.md`). The
clear text was known before the decipherment started, so this reading is NOT independent of it: it is a sign-by-sign
verification of f. 63 against the duplicate.

Sign names: as key.md / f30.md / f37.md. Slip shapes: c = h with a hook at the foot (ħ); l = h-like sign with a top
bar or flourish (ŧ); i = plain barred 5 or b or 6-hook; el = b with a short top bar (ƀ); er = b with a bar over the
bowl (b̄); es = b with an E-like top bar (E-barred-b); v/u = 8 or ƀ̿; et = 5/ʒ-like sign (5); at = b-tail; s = ɱ (on
this slip a y/ɣ-like sign with a tail, "ɱ") or ʃ.

## Line by line

= sexto Marcii et duplicata die eodem. Dux Mediolani et cet[er]a. {padding}

## Plaintext (word division mine)

Siamo stati avisati da Foscino et da Contuzo, et rechesti che gli voglamo dare favore per dubio che hano de le gente de
li inimici, le quale sono adunate et pasate de za da Oglio in Cremonese, et intendano andare ad Cremona per torre
quella terra; et questo medesemo havemo sentito da molti et diversi altri lochi, per la quale cosa havemo subito
mandato in quelle parte Mesere Carlo, Mesere Alberto di Pii, e'l Conte Carlo con le gente loro; havemoli etiam mandato
Mesere Biaxo de Azareto con l'armata per aqua, et havemo comiso ad Petro Vesconte chi è ad Piziguitone, et ad Zorzo de
Anono [sic], el quale è ad nome nostro con le dicte [Gente d'arme], che ad ogni richesta de queli vostri vadano in loro
aiuto et favore, et per salvarve quella terra fazano tuto quello et come sarà bisogno, et come fariano per la persona
nostra propria; et così ad ogni nostra posanza se inzegneremo et perforzaremo de dargli ogni subsidio et socorso,
perché ve la posano salvare. Ad che ve ne havemo voluto avisare, perché sapiati el tuto et in che termini stano li
facti vostri de Cremona. Avisandove che se ne fusemo stati avisati ad bona hora haveresemo ben proveduto per modo che
non solamente non se sariano metuti ad questo, ma non pur ad pensarlo. Mediolani sexto Marcii, et duplicata die eodem.
Dux Mediolani et cet[er]a.

## Summary

The Duke tells Sforza that Foscino and Contuzo have warned him and asked for help: the enemy (Venetian) troops have
gathered and crossed the Oglio into the Cremonese and mean to take Cremona. He has sent Carlo [Gonzaga], Alberto Pio and
Count Carlo with their men, Biagio d'Assereto with the river fleet, and ordered Pietro Visconti at Pizzighettone and
Giorgio d'Annone, with the men-at-arms, to go to the aid of Sforza's people whenever asked and to do all they would do
for the Duke himself; he will give every help so that the city is saved, and regrets he was not warned in time, when he
could have prevented the attempt altogether. Duplicate of the letter of 6 March 1447 (f. 64), sent the same day.

## Comparison with the later clear text f. 63

All of f. 63 is on the slip, in the same order. Differences (slip vs f. 63):

1. L1 first informant: the slip spells **F-os-c-in-o = "Foscino"** (S par o## ∂÷ y) where f. 63 has "Fesino" (read also
   "Tescino"). f. 64 spells the same name "Fosceo" (S par o## ÷ y): the two slips agree on F-os-c-, differ in the end.
2. L1 second informant: **Con-t-uz-o = "Contuzo"** (6 g ϕo ꭓ), as f. 63 and f. 64.
3. L4 "per torre": t | or | b̄ | e; the b̄ (normally er) is read r [?]: the slip may have "torere". f. 63 "torre".
4. L6 a null (x with a stroke and a dot) between "parte" and "Mesere Carlo" (f. 64 has a null at the same place).
5. L9 **"Piziguitone"** (b tt 8 fa = i g u it) vs f. 63 "Pizighitone" (f. 64 the same as f. 65).
6. L9 **"Anono"**: y (= o) where e is expected ("de Anon[o]") vs f. 63 "Anone". Read y = o [?]; f. 64 has "Anone".
7. L10 "gente d'arme" is the name sign ÷9 (Soldati / Gente d'arme); "le gente" (L2) and "le gente loro" (L7) spelled out.
8. L10 "richesta" (∂-barred = ic) vs f. 63 "rechesta".
9. L11 "in loro aiuto" vs "in lor ajuto".
10. L11 "quella terra" vs "quela terra" (L4 "quella terra" agrees); L12 "quello" (second l [?]) vs "quelo".
11. L13-14 "se inzegneremo" (separate s-e; ƀ = er in -neremo) vs "s'inzegnaremo".
12. L16 "el tuto et in che termini" agrees with f. 63 (f. 64 omits "et").
13. Date: **no "Data" and no "die"**: the slip has "pensarlo. Mediolani sexto Marcii, et duplicata die eodem. Dux
    Mediolani et cet[er]a." That is word for word f. 63's "Altra simile -- Mediolani sexto Martii, et duplicata die
    eodem": the duplicate note of f. 63 comes from this slip. "Marcii" (o## = c) vs "Martii". The er of "cetera" is not
    seen. f. 63's "Data Mediolani die sexto Martii" is the f. 64 date.
14. After "et cetera": 8 signs that give no sense (not an obvious alphabet run) and a π null. No "1447" on the slip.

## Comparison with the original f. 64 (texts/f64.md)

Same letter, same order, same spellings of the other names (Oglio, Cremonese, Cremona, Mesere Carlo, Mesere Alberto di
Pii, Conte Carlo, Mesere Biaxo de Azareto, Petro Vesconte, Piziguitone, Zorzo), the ÷9 sign at the same place, the
perché sign twice, the null before Mesere Carlo, "Marcii" in both. Differences (f. 65 vs f. 64):

- "Foscino" (S par o## ∂÷ y) vs "Fosceo" (S par o## ÷ y).
- "per torre" t-or-?-e vs "per [t]orre" (f. 64 y for t).
- "Anono" vs "Anone"; "con le dicte" vs "con l[e] dicte".
- "richesta" vs "rechesta"; "loro aiuto" vs "lor aiuto"; "quella terra" (L11) vs "quela terra"; "quello" vs "quelo";
  "se inzegneremo" vs "s'inzegnaremo".
- "el tuto et in che" vs "el tuto in che" (f. 64 drops et).
- f. 64 has "et" and v inserted above the line ("favore [et] per", "salvar[v]e"); f. 65 has both in the line.
- Date: f. 65 "Mediolani sexto Marcii, et duplicata die eodem" vs f. 64 "Data Mediolani die sexto Marcii".
- Padding: f. 65 8 signs, f. 64 a 22-sign alphabet run in key order. f. 65 has 20 lines, f. 64 17.

## Read share

From `f65_tokens.tsv` (`python texts/mk_tokens.py 65`): 20 lines, 703 tokens, 3 nulls, 8 unread (the padding after
"et cetera"), 20 read with [?]. Non-null tokens read: 692/700 = 98.9%. All of the letter text is read; open points:
the sign read r in "torre" (L4), y = o in "Anono" (L9), the p and the small o of "pur" (L19), t in "sexto" (L20), the
missing er of "cetera", and single small "+" read i (L5, L7, L13, L16).

# BnF italien 1584 f. 65 -- Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, duplicate (6 Mar 1447) of the letter of 6 Mar 1447 (cipher slip): reading

Siamo stati avisati da Foscino et da Contuzo, et rechesti che gli voglamo dare favore per dubio che hano de le gente de
li inimici, le quale sono adunate et pasate de za da Oglio in Cremonese, et intendano andare ad Cremona per torre
quella terra; et questo medesemo havemo sentito da molti et diversi altri lochi, per la quale cosa havemo subito
mandato in quelle parte Mesere Carlo, Mesere Alberto di Pii, e'l Conte Carlo con le gente loro; havemoli etiam mandato
Mesere Biaxo de Azareto con l'armata per aqua, et havemo comiso ad Petro Vesconte chi è ad Piziguitone, et ad Zorzo de
Anono [sic], el quale è ad nome nostro con le dicte [Gente d'arme], che ad ogni richesta de queli vostri vadano in loro
aiuto et favore, et per salvarve quella terra fazano tuto quello et come sarà bisogno, et come fariano per la persona
nostra propria; et così ad ogni nostra posanza se inzegneremo et perforzaremo de dargli ogni subsidio et socorso,
perché ve la posano salvare. Ad che ve ne havemo voluto avisare, perché sapiati el tuto et in che termini stano li
facti vostri de Cremona. Avisandove che se ne fusemo stati avisati ad bona hora haveresemo ben proveduto per modo che
non solamente non se sariano metuti ad questo, ma non pur ad pensarlo. Mediolani sexto Marcii, et duplicata die eodem.
Dux Mediolani et cet[er]a.
Translation

f65_en.md

# BnF italien 1584 f. 65 -- Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, duplicate (6 Mar 1447) of the letter of 6 Mar 1447 (cipher slip): English

The Duke tells Sforza that Foscino and Contuzo have warned him and asked for help: the enemy (Venetian) troops have
gathered and crossed the Oglio into the Cremonese and mean to take Cremona. He has sent Carlo [Gonzaga], Alberto Pio and
Count Carlo with their men, Biagio d'Assereto with the river fleet, and ordered Pietro Visconti at Pizzighettone and
Giorgio d'Annone, with the men-at-arms, to go to the aid of Sforza's people whenever asked and to do all they would do
for the Duke himself; he will give every help so that the city is saved, and regrets he was not warned in time, when he
could have prevented the attempt altogether. Duplicate of the letter of 6 March 1447 (f. 64), sent the same day.

f. 72, Milan, 7 Mar 1447 read in part

Answer to four briefs of Sforza and a letter of Marcolino Barbavara: on binding himself to the Church and the King of Aragon Sforza should do as seems best and tell Marcolino; Pietro da Pusterla has gone to Ferrara; a request brought by Angelo Simonetta.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense; the holes are marked in the reading.

## Continuous plaintext (Lombard spellings kept; [..] unread, [?] uncertain)

[Qua?]tro vostri brevi, dato l'uno ad dece octo et le altri a vinteuno del pasato, e cosi come [..] ha scripto da
p[..]o[?] Marcolino Barbavara. Havemo inteso [..] [..] et cose, capitoli[?] et ricorde vostri, et respondendo a quele
parte rechedeno reposta, dicemo: a la parte de obligarve al Papa et al [Re de Ragona?] che nui se [..] pigliati [..]
parte e parira[..] de obligarve a la chiesa et al [Re de Ragona?] in quela forma ve parira, come quelo tempo che son
[con]tenti, contr[..]ire a la spesa vostra overo che solamente [..] obligato a aver, come [..] promet[..]o a loro. Per
vui[?] che non li ofenderiti, et de quelo partito pigliariti[?] [..] subito avisare el dicto Marcolino, quanto a vi[?]
gli havemo [..]miso, che [..] faza et segua quelo partito. [..] avisariti come li facte[?] del [Duca di Ferrara?] et
del [name sign <]. Havemo mandato a Ferrara Petro da Pusterla per altre nostre lettere. Habiamo scripto, et cosi
habiamo scripto a Roma et al [Re de Ragona?] [..] Roberto. De Angelo Simonetta vostro secretario havemo inteso que[lo]
che scriviti, et se sforzaremo satisfare a la rechiesta vostra [..] de Iacomino [..]. [..] respondemo che ve ne
compiacemo volonti[era] et non solamente de l[..] de [..] [..]ra cosa [..] da [..]vi. Dato Mediolani septimo Marcii.
[Dux Mediolani] et cetera. [closing group of padding signs]

## English summary (gist)

The Duke acknowledges four [?] briefs of Sforza's, one dated the 18th and the others the 21st of last month, and what
Marcolino Barbavara has written. On the points Sforza asks to be answered: as to his binding himself to the Pope (i.e.
the Church) and to the King of Aragon [?], Sforza should do so in whatever form seems best to him, at his own expense,
or only as far as he is obliged; for his part, provided he does not harm them, Sforza should at once tell Marcolino
which course he takes, so that Marcolino may follow it, and report on the affairs of [Ferrara?] and of another
(unlabelled name sign). The Duke has sent Pietro da Pusterla to Ferrara with other letters and has written to Rome and
to the King [?]. He has heard from Sforza's secretary Angelo Simonetta what Sforza writes and will try to satisfy his
request concerning one Iacomino; he is glad to do so. Milan, 7 March [1447].

Context: Pope Eugene IV had died on 23 Feb 1447 and Nicholas V was elected on 6 Mar; Barbavara was the Duke's envoy and
Pusterla was writing to the Duke from Ferrara on the same day (f. 71). The reading of the δ÷ name sign as Re de Ragona
(as on f. 102) rather than Duca di Ferrara (the copy gives both labels to near-identical shapes) is not settled; in L9
the sign is drawn ꝺ+÷, beside "a Ferrara", and is read Duca di Ferrara [?].

## Open points

- L1-L3: the stretch "come [ꝯo 8 ħ̄ o+ | ·‖· 8 ħ υ ⊢]" (perhaps damaged at the left edge), the surname-like group before
  Marcolino (∴ ƀ̄ ħ̄ β, "da P..o"), and the clause "ħ ħ̄ ∂ ħ̿ pı ꝗ̇ bı 9 b ‖ ʒ ⊢ ‡ b= | ŧ 8 c∞ 9 b" after "havemo inteso"
  (c-i-r-c-on-?-ent-i...?) are unread.
- L4 "che nui se [ꝗ y b y bı gtt b] pigliati [g-ʃ ħ ·‖·] parte": the verb is not read.
- L6 "contr[∂ʒ 8]ire" and "solamente [∂ p÷ g÷ı υ ħ- b]" (Romagna...?), L7 opening.
- L11 opening (non[?] ... the 'est' name sign, which the key labels S. Malatesti, and "Roberto"): construction unclear.
- L12-L13 "[ꝗ̃] de Iacomino [g | ÷ o+ ƀ̄ σ- ꝯ ·‖·]" and L13-L14 "de l[..] ... [..]ra cosa": unread.
- Closing group after "et cetera": ÷ β b + ħ̄ β ꭓ y ♀ π ff, padding (see `closing_groups.md`), left as [..] here.

## Counts

713 tokens: 605 read (26 of them [?]), 10 nulls, 98 unread = 86.1% of non-null tokens read (mkmd). The share overstates the sense read: about 40 of the 'read' tokens carry a letter value inside a word not yet identified (L2-L4, L6, L13-L14). Leaving out the 10 padding signs of the closing group, 98-10 = 88 text tokens are unread.

f72_en.md

# BnF italien 1584 f. 72: cipher slip, Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, Milan 7 Mar 1447: English

The Duke acknowledges four [?] briefs of Sforza's, one dated the 18th and the others the 21st of last month, and what
Marcolino Barbavara has written. On the points Sforza asks to be answered: as to his binding himself to the Pope (i.e.
the Church) and to the King of Aragon [?], Sforza should do so in whatever form seems best to him, at his own expense,
or only as far as he is obliged; for his part, provided he does not harm them, Sforza should at once tell Marcolino
which course he takes, so that Marcolino may follow it, and report on the affairs of [Ferrara?] and of another
(unlabelled name sign). The Duke has sent Pietro da Pusterla to Ferrara with other letters and has written to Rome and
to the King [?]. He has heard from Sforza's secretary Angelo Simonetta what Sforza writes and will try to satisfy his
request concerning one Iacomino; he is glad to do so. Milan, 7 March [1447].

Context: Pope Eugene IV had died on 23 Feb 1447 and Nicholas V was elected on 6 Mar; Barbavara was the Duke's envoy and
Pusterla was writing to the Duke from Ferrara on the same day (f. 71). The reading of the δ÷ name sign as Re de Ragona
(as on f. 102) rather than Duca di Ferrara (the copy gives both labels to near-identical shapes) is not settled; in L9
the sign is drawn ꝺ+÷, beside "a Ferrara", and is read Duca di Ferrara [?].

f. 83, Milan, 11 Mar 1447

The Dauphin and Asti through Tommaso da Bologna; Rome and the Cardinal Camerlengo; Padua or Verona offered to the Duke of Ferrara; the Malatesta condotte; the Count of Urbino's planned raid; discontent in Milan with the agreement.

Transcription

# BnF italien 1584 f. 83: cipher slip, Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, Milan 11 Mar 1447 (SAMPLE LINES)

Status: **sample transcription only** (lines 1-3, first half of 20, 36-37: 274 of roughly 2,700 signs). The slip is the
largest of the set: 37 long lines written across the leaf (canvas 80, right page, rotated 90 deg on the mount). Image
`img/c80.jpg` cropped (4500,480)-(7380,4560), rotated 270 deg, deskewed +1.1 deg = `img/s83_desk.jpg`; line zooms
`img/hl/f83_L01a-L37d.jpg` (`tools_A/halves.py`, peaks listed in `f83_work.txt`). Key **R5323** (line 1 `ꝗo ‖ ...
‡ b= b° y ꝯ bʒ bʒ ẋ ꭓ` = "Per G... havemo recevuto"). Conventions as `f8.md`. Worker C, 29 Oct 2026.

Each sample line was deciphered sign by sign from the key before being set against the clear text `f83_clear.md`
(the neat later copy on ff. 82r-82v, 85r). The remaining 31 lines were not transcribed (time); every part looked at
follows the clear text word for word, so the work left is mechanical alignment, not decipherment.

## Comparison with the clear copy (`f83_clear.md`)

| cipher line | clear text (ff. 82r-85r) | result |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | Per Gasparro de li Ubaldini havemo recevuto uno vostro breve dato al ultimo del pasato, et quelo ben in- | all words present, same order; "Gasparro" and "Ubaldini" spelled with signs not in the key (ħ ∵, σ- ꝗ ꝯ), read only by the clear text |
| L2 | -teso ve respondemo. Et primamente a la parte del Dalfino et cetera: che nui se intendemo optimamente con si | same, except the cipher has `ƀ̇ ⊢` where the copy has "-teso ve" (one or two words fewer in the cipher, or ƀ̇ a group sign); end of line (con si) faded |
| L3 | et havemo bona inteligentia insiema, et per quelo havemo havuto da Thomase da Bologna, el quale | same; "Bologna" partly read |
| L20 (half) | ...voriti. A la parte de li Signori Malatesti, come per altre | same |
| L36-37 | Datum Mediolani die undecimo Martii 1447. Filipus Maria Anglus Dux Mediolani et cetera, Papie Anglerieque Comes ac Ianue Dominus. | same words; **the cipher has no year** (die undecimo martii, then nulls), the copy adds "1447"; the cipher's closing group of 11 signs after the title is not in the copy |

Of the 237 read tokens in the sample, all agree with the clear text except the L2 `ƀ̇ ⊢` against "teso ve" (counted as 2
disagreements): **235/237 = 99.2%**. The 23 unread tokens are the faded end of L2-L3, one sign in the date and the
closing group. **Conclusion: the neat copy is the text of f. 83** (same wording and order, including Lombard spellings
of the cipher such as "insiema", "Thomase", "recevuto"), with the year supplied by the copyist and the closing group
left out. The hand is the 18th-c.-style italic of ff. 29, 35, 63 (worker B), not contemporary: no abbreviation,
modern accents and punctuation, Latin "Datum". It is therefore a prior decipherment (or a copy of one) made long after
1447, presumably from a key of the R5323 family.

## English summary (from the clear copy, confirmed in the sample)

By Gasparro degli Ubaldini we received your brief of the last of last month. On the Dauphin: we understand each other
perfectly; Tommaso da Bologna, our man, should have concluded and sealed with him on the first of this month; we wrote
that he should conclude if he had not, telling him we will give the Dauphin Asti; we have his promise not to be
attacked by him, and he is bound to help us with a good number of horse and foot (how many and for how long we do not
yet know). We approve your sending to the Cardinal Camerlengo and to the man from Capua; tell us what you want done at
the court of Rome and we will do it as for ourselves. On offering Padua or Verona to the Duke of Ferrara: as written
before, that is your business; offer what you think fit and we will confirm it; Pietro da Pusterla has gone to Ferrara
with that commission and to press the business of the lord of Faenza [later gloss]. On the Malatesta lords: we have
offered Malatesta his present condotta and Sigismondo 1,600 horse, and can do no more except through the Church. We
keep our agreement secret and will not fail over the money. We thank you for Ser Angelo's warning of the Count of
Urbino's planned raid towards Milan; as for watchfulness in Milan and the other cities, we do what we can, but some
here are unhappy with our agreement and we can hardly guard against friends and enemies: advise us what to do, for we
will follow your counsel. Milan, 11 March [1447].

## Counts

Sample: 274 tokens, 237 read (14 of them [?]), 14 nulls, 23 unread (91.2% of non-null tokens). Whole slip: about 8%
transcribed.
Decryption

# BnF italien 1584 ff. 82r, 82v, 85r (foliation as pencilled): later clear text of the cipher slip f. 83, Milan 11 Mar 1447

Transcribed 29 Oct 2026 (worker C) from canvases 79 right page (f. 82r, numbered "82"), 80 left page (its verso) and 82
right page (numbered "85"), ark btv1b100373864. One neat italic hand, the same as the 18th-c.-style clear texts of
ff. 29, 35 and 63 found by worker B (`clear_ff29_35_63.md`): no abbreviations, accents on "perché", "è", "più", "sì",
colons at line-end word breaks (":"), Latin date line "Datum Mediolani". Head in a later archivist's hand: "1447. 11 Mars".
In the margin of f. 82v, l. 4, a later pencil gloss over "S." reads "di Faenz(a)" ("el facto del S. di Faenza").
Line breaks of the clear pages kept as `/`; word-break colons dropped. Spellings as written.

## f. 82r (canvas 79, right page)

Per Gasparro de li Ubaldini havemo recevuto uno vostro breve dato / al ultimo del pasato, et quelo ben inteso ve
respondemo. / Et primamente a la parte del Dalfino et cetera: che nui / se intendemo optimamente con si, et havemo bona
inteli- / gentia insiema, et per quelo havemo havuto da Thoma- / se da Bologna, el quale è per nui, da si el debe havere
/ concluso et sigilato con si a calende del mese presente, / perché 'l ne ha scripto, che senza expectare altra res- /
posta da nui el concluderia el dicto dì con si, et nui ni- / entedimeno gli havemo scripto subito, chel concluda, se /
ancora el non havese concluso, avisandone che nui gli da- / simo Ast, et havemo promesa et secureza da lui de non /
recevere offesa alcuna da si, anci el è obligato aiutarne / et proverirne de una bona somma de soldati da cavalo et /
da pede, la quale non sapemo specificamente quanto la se / sia, né per quanto tempo, perché ancora non era concluso /
come è dicto, ma ne expectamo la chiareza, et quela ha- / vuta subito ve ne avisaremo. A la parte del havere /
mandato dal Cardinale Camerlengo, et da quelo da Capua, / et cetera, ne piace, et molto bene commendiamo et ringra- /
tiamo, et parene non havesti potuto fare meglio, et se / ve pare o piace, che nui in corte da Roma faciamo / più una
cosa che un'altra per gi, voglatine subito avi- / sare, perché la faremo volentera et de bona vogla co- / me per nui
stesi, et più volentera che per nui stesi. A / la parte del offerire al Duca di Ferara Padua o Verona / dicemo, come
per altre nostre ve habiamo scripto, che / queste volemo et intendemo sia vostro carico, et che / gli offerati, o
faciati offerire quelo et come ve parirà,

## f. 82v (canvas 80, left page)

et piacere, perché de quelo che fariti remaneremo contenti, / et lo confirmaremo, et così ha in comissione Pedro de
Pusterla / da nui, el quale havemo mandato a Ferrara per solicitare et / strenzere el facto del S. [gloss: di Faenza]
in quela forma che gi vo- / riti, perché gli havemo commeso, che de questo el se ne / intenda con gi, et faga quelo et
come voriti. A la parte de / li Signori Malatesti, come per altre nostre ve habiamo scri- / pto, nui gli habiamo facto
et faciamo quelo havemo posu- / to, et posemo, avisandove che havemo proferto al Signore / Malatesta quela conducta chel
ha havuta fino a qui con / nui, et al Signore Sigismondo mille seicento cavali, come per / altre nostre ve havemo
scripto, sì che a questo non sapiamo / come altramente provedere, salvo che se inzegnaremo et / perforzaremo provederli
per la via de Sancta Chiesa. Del te- / nire secreto l'acordo vostro con nui ve avisamo, che nui / dal canto nostro
l'havemo facto et lo faremo più ne sarà / posibile, et così circa el retrare del dinaro non mancaremo / in cosa alcuna,
né se gli perde tempo. A la parte del aviso / ve ha dato Ser Angelo de la cavalcata era ordinata che facese / el Conte
d'Urbino verso Milano, ve ne ringratiamo quanto più / savemo et posemo, et così similmente el dicto Ser Angelo, ma / gi
haveti potuto cognoscere se l'hano voluta fare verso Mi- / lano o altrove, et perché ne scriviti che voglamo havere bona
/ advertentia in Milano et in le altre nostre citade, et in li / Capitanei et cetera, ve respondemo che nui l'havevemo
in / Milano meglore potevemo, ma ben ve recordiamo, come al- / tre volte ve habiamo avisato, che con nui sono de queli
che / sono malcontenti del acordo facto tra gi et nui, et nui male / se potemo guardare et deffendere da amici et
inimici, sì

## f. 85r (canvas 82, right page)

che ve pregamo ne voglati conseglare ad lo ve pare che ha- / biamo a fare, perché se perforzaremo fare secondo li
conseglii / vostri. / Datum Mediolani die undecimo Martii 1447. / Filipus Maria Anglus Dux Mediolani et cetera, Papie,
An- / glerieque Comes ac Ianue Dominus.

# BnF italien 1584 f. 83: cipher slip, Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, Milan 11 Mar 1447 (SAMPLE LINES): English

By Gasparro degli Ubaldini we received your brief of the last of last month. On the Dauphin: we understand each other
perfectly; Tommaso da Bologna, our man, should have concluded and sealed with him on the first of this month; we wrote
that he should conclude if he had not, telling him we will give the Dauphin Asti; we have his promise not to be
attacked by him, and he is bound to help us with a good number of horse and foot (how many and for how long we do not
yet know). We approve your sending to the Cardinal Camerlengo and to the man from Capua; tell us what you want done at
the court of Rome and we will do it as for ourselves. On offering Padua or Verona to the Duke of Ferrara: as written
before, that is your business; offer what you think fit and we will confirm it; Pietro da Pusterla has gone to Ferrara
with that commission and to press the business of the lord of Faenza [later gloss]. On the Malatesta lords: we have
offered Malatesta his present condotta and Sigismondo 1,600 horse, and can do no more except through the Church. We
keep our agreement secret and will not fail over the money. We thank you for Ser Angelo's warning of the Count of
Urbino's planned raid towards Milan; as for watchfulness in Milan and the other cities, we do what we can, but some
here are unhappy with our agreement and we can hardly guard against friends and enemies: advise us what to do, for we
will follow your counsel. Milan, 11 March [1447].

f. 94, Milan, 18 Mar 1447

The Duke is glad Sforza sent Messer Nicola Maffeo; praises his letters to the College of Cardinals and the governor of the March and the truce with the Malatesta; asks how he should write from now on.

## Comparison with the clear copy (`f94_clear.md`)

| cipher | clear copy | agreement |
|---|---|---|
| L1 Dapoiché G[asp]arro de li Ubaldini e zonto **qui** havemo recevuto uno vostro brev- | Dapoiché Gasparro de li Ubaldini è zonto havemo recevuto uno vostro breve | all but `gto` = "qui", which the copy omits |
| L2 -e per lo quale ne scriviti tra le altre cose de Messere Nicola **Ma[ff]eo** che mand- | per lo quale ne scriviti tra le altre cose de Mesere Nicola **Mageo**, che mandasti | same, except the copy's "Mageo" where the cipher has `c ħ̊ ÷ β` (m-?-e-o; ħ̊ not "g", most likely "ff": Maffeo) |
| L3 -asiti qui da nui. Onde respondendo ve dicemo chel ne piace grandemen- | mandasti qui da nui, onde respondendo ve dicemo chel ne piace grandemente | same; cipher "mandasiti" (`5̄ fa ⊢` as-it-i) against "mandasti" |
| L19-21 ...die decimo octavo marti-i mile quatrocento quarantasete. Filip[L]us Maria Anglus Dux Mediolani etcetera, Papie Anglerieque comes ac Ianue dominus. + closing group | Data Mediolani die decimo octavo Martii, mille quatrocento quarantasette. Filipus Maria Anglus Dux Mediolani et cetera, Papie Anglerieque Comes ac Ianue Dominus. | same words, year in words in both (the copy follows the cipher here: compare f. 83, where the cipher has no year and the copy adds "1447" in figures); spelling mile/mille, quarantasete/-sette; closing group not in the copy |

Of the 205 read tokens of the sample, 202 agree with the clear text; the 3 disagreements are `gto` (qui, omitted by the
copy), `ħ̊` (ff/g in Maffeo/Mageo) and `fa` (mandasiti/mandasti): **202/205 = 98.5%**. Single/double consonant variants
(mile/mille, contentisimi) are not counted.

**Conclusion: the neat copy (f. 92 in Mazzatinti, "copia moderna: manca l'originale") is the text of f. 94**: the
"missing original" is the cipher slip two leaves on, of which the copy is a decipherment. The copy is not
contemporary: same 18th-c.-style italic as ff. 29, 35, 63, 82 (no abbreviations, modern accents, punctuation and
capitals). Its one name slip ("Mageo" for the cipher's Maffeo) and the dropped "qui" show it was made from the
cipher (or from a working decipherment), not from a clear original.

## English summary (from the clear copy, confirmed in the sample)

Now that Gasparro degli Ubaldini has arrived we have your brief about Messer Nicola Maffeo [copy: Mageo], whom you sent
here. We are very glad and content you sent him; of all those outside your household no one's coming could please us
more, for we have always known him as a worthy man devoted to you, wanting nothing but that you and we be one. We
commend your conduct in writing to the College of Cardinals and the governor of the March, in stopping hostilities and
making the truce with the Malatesta lords: you have acted most prudently, we thank you and ask you to persevere. No
time is lost over raising the money. Tell us how we should write to you from now on: by simple briefs as before, or
with superscriptions, subscriptions and seals, and we will do as you say. Milan, 18 March 1447.

## Counts

Sample: 223 tokens, 205 read (4 [?]), 7 nulls, 11 unread (the closing group) = 94.9% of non-null tokens; about 18% of the
slip transcribed.
Decryption

# BnF italien 1584 f. 92 (Mazzatinti; pencilled "93" on the leaf): later clear text of the cipher slip f. 94, Milan 18 Mar 1447

Mazzatinti: "Lettera del Duca (Milano 18 marzo). Copia moderna: manca l'originale". Transcribed 29 Oct 2026 (worker C)
from canvas 90 (ark btv1b100373864), right-hand leaf, pencil folio "93" (Mazzatinti's count is one lower here). The
text fills the right column of the recto; the date and title stand as a separate block lower left on the same opening
(canvas 90, x 2250-2950, y 1350-1700 at half resolution); the verso (canvas 91 left page) is blank, showing only
show-through. Same neat italic hand as `f83_clear.md` and worker B's ff. 29, 35, 63. Head in the archivist's hand:
"1447. 18 Mars". Line breaks kept as `/`; word-break colons dropped.

Dapoiché Gasparro de li Ubaldini è zonto havemo recevuto uno / vostro breve, per lo quale ne scriviti tra le altre cose
/ de Mesere Nicola Mageo, che mandasti qui da nui, / onde respondendo ve dicemo chel ne piace grandem- / ente, et siamo
contentisimi lo mandati, et da que- / li chi fuseno de casa vostra in fora ne piace più / la venuta soa, che del alcun
altro, perché nui l'ha- / vemo sempre cognosuto valente homo et molto / affectionato a gi, et come affectionato chel era
non / desiderare altro, nomà che gi et nui fuseno una me- / desma cosa insieme. De li modi haveti servato così / in
scrivere al Colegio de li Cardinali et al Governato- / re de la Marcha, come in havere levato le offese / et havere
facto la treugua con li S.ri Malatesti / ve commendiamo grandemente, et parene ve siati / diportato prudentisimamente et
bene, de che ve / ringratiamo quanto più savemo et posemo, et pre- / gameve voglati perseverare così per l'avenire. /
Circa lo retrare li denari non se perde tempo, et / se farà cum effecto quanto bisogna, come per altre / nostre ve
habiamo scripto. Apreso ve pregamo ne / avisati, quando da qui inance ne accaderà a scri- / verve, in che forma ve
debiamo fare scrivere, o / per brevi simplici come havemo facto per lo pasato, / overo con li soprascripti et
sotoscripti et sigillati, / perché secondo ne scriveriti nui faremo exequire.

Data Mediolani die decimo octavo Martii, mille / quatrocento quarantasette. / Filipus Maria Anglus Dux Mediolani et
cetera, / Papie Anglerieque Comes ac Ianue Dominus.

# BnF italien 1584 f. 94: cipher slip, Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, Milan 18 Mar 1447 (SAMPLE LINES): English

Now that Gasparro degli Ubaldini has arrived we have your brief about Messer Nicola Maffeo [copy: Mageo], whom you sent
here. We are very glad and content you sent him; of all those outside your household no one's coming could please us
more, for we have always known him as a worthy man devoted to you, wanting nothing but that you and we be one. We
commend your conduct in writing to the College of Cardinals and the governor of the March, in stopping hostilities and
making the truce with the Malatesta lords: you have acted most prudently, we thank you and ask you to persevere. No
time is lost over raising the money. Tell us how we should write to you from now on: by simple briefs as before, or
with superscriptions, subscriptions and seals, and we will do as you say. Milan, 18 March 1447.

f. 102, Milan, 23 Mar 1447 (duplicate) read in part

The new pope, Nicholas V, will not do what he ought; Sforza should come to an understanding with the King of Aragon and act before the pope sets his feet more firmly.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense; the holes are marked in the reading.

Canvas 98, upper slip of the right-hand mount (pencil "102"); IIIF region `.../f98/4050,980,3550,800/max/0/default.jpg`

sign. Key **R5323** (l. 1 `o## 5̿ 2p ∂+ ·‖·` = "carissime", `∂÷ gtt bı +o b° β` = "intendemo"; the name signs looped δo
= Papa and looped δ÷ = Re de Ragona of the R5323 copy). Conventions as `f8.md`. Worker C, 29 Oct 2026; deciphered from
the key, then compared with the clear letter f. 103 below it (`f102_clear.md`). No ducal title at the end: the slip
closes with the date and a second dated group.

## Comparison with f. 103 (`f102_clear.md`)

The cipher and f. 103 carry the same message clause by clause, in the same order, but f. 103 is **not** a
decipherment of f. 102 word for word: it is the formal clear letter of the same day (Latin address, chancery
abbreviations, "Datum die xxiij Martii 1447"), and the cipher is a shorter Lombard version:

| clause | f. 102 (cipher) | f. 103 (clear) |
|---|---|---|
| address | Illustris[?] fili noster carissime | Illustris fili noster carissime |
| the pope | pur **el novo Papa** (name sign ꝺo) non inclinarse ad volere fare que[l] debe | pur **la S.tà de N. S. el papa** non inclinarse ad volere fare quello se debe |
| advice | dicemo che **a nui pare** et cosi ve confortiamo et pregamo ve voglate intendere **[con] Re de Ragona** (name sign ꝺ÷) | dicemo che **ad noy par** et cossi ve confortiamo et pregamo ve vogliate intendere **cum la M.tà del Re de Ragona** |
| | al[qua]le havemo scripto el simile, e quando **vedereti** esso papa non volere fare quel debe, [che] desidera esso Re et vui | alquale havemo scripto el simile, et quando **vedati** esso papa non volere fare quello chel debe, et che desidera esso Re et vuy |
| | intendandove la Maiest[?]ate soa et vui insieme ve as[..] talmente de li facti soi et provediati e faciati con effecto | intendendove la M.tà sua et voy insieme ve assecurarete talmente dele facti suoy, et provediati et fazati con effecto |
| | **inanci** che esso papa habia piu **firmato** li pedi, per modo et per manera che se ben [esso papa volesse sinis]trare non posa | che **anzi** che esso papa habia piu **firmati** li pedi, per modo et per manera che se ben esso papa volesse sinistrar non possa |
| date | Data Mediolani vigesimo tertio martii, then "et duplicata [..] vigesimo [..]" (duplicate note, worker E) | Datum die xxiij Martij 1447 |

Token match: of the 279 read cipher tokens, about 262 have their counterpart in f. 103 at the same place; the
differences are wording, not reading errors: "el novo" (5 tokens, against "la S.tà de N. S."), name signs for "la M.tà
del Re", "vedereti/vedati", "inanci/anzi", "firmato/firmati", "Mediolani" (6 tokens) and the second date (not in
f. 103). **Match about 94% (262/279)**, against a clear text that is a parallel letter, not a copy. The struck "pe-"
before "pedi" in f. 103 and the "13" for xxiii in the archivist's heading are slips of the clear letter.

**Conclusion**: f. 103 is a contemporary (1447) clear letter of the same day and content, probably the chancery's fair
version or a covering letter, written by a secretary from the same minute as the cipher; it is the clear text of
f. 102 in substance but not a decipherment. The cipher's "el novo Papa" dates the letter after the election of
Nicholas V (6 Mar 1447); the clear version names him with his title.

## English summary

Most dear son: since we understand that the new Pope is not inclined to do what he should, we advise and urge you to
come to an understanding with the King of Aragon, to whom we have written the same; and when you see that the Pope will
not do what he should and what the King and you desire, you and His Majesty together should make sure of his affairs
and act effectively before the Pope has set his feet more firmly, so that even if he wished to do harm he could not.
Milan, 23 March [1447].

## Worker E pass (29 Oct 2026): gaps re-examined, f. 103 used as a guide to sense only

Each gap re-read at the hl zooms; a value was accepted only when the slip's signs give it with the key or with values
established on other slips, f. 103 serving to say which word to look for. Changes (old -> new):

1. L2 start "[..] debe" -> "[e][?] debe": the edge sign is ·‖· (cut); "que e debe" / "que debe" (f. 103 "quello se debe").
2. L2 "ve [..]l[i]at[i] intend-" -> "ve voglate intend-": `ƀ̄ p- ħ ħ- ⊢` = v-og-l-at-e (p- = og, the key's og; the old
   list had ħ ħ ⊢, the second is ħ- = at). Lombard "voglate" (f. 103 "vogliate"; f. 31 has "voglati").
3. L3 "al[..]le" -> "al[qu]a[?]le": `8̶ ♀ ꝑ ħ ÷`; ♀ = qu and ꝑ = a only from f. 103 "alquale" [?].
4. **L4 the 15-sign gap** -> "la Maiest[?]ate soa et vui": `5̄ q- σ- b ƀ̄ y b- ⊢ g ꭓ σ- ħ ƀ 8 ʒ` = l-am-a i-es-[t]-at-e
   s-o-a et v-u-i; q- = am (barred q), g = s (long s), ħ = et, ʒ = i (the key's i = 3). y for t is the one misfit [?].
   Same words as f. 103 "la M.tà sua et voy", in Lombard "soa", "vui".
5. L5 "soi[..] provediati" -> "soi et provediati" (ħ = et, the unbarred-5 et); "de li [f]acti": the sign is ∂- = if
   ("l-if-ac-t-i", as B's "li facti" on f. 64); "faciati [con] effecto" -> "con effecto": a faint small 6 = con.
6. L6 "-a [..] piu" -> "-abia piu": `co b ♀` = ab-i-a; with L5's `∴ ft` the run is p-ap-ab-i-a, "papa (h)abia" with
   the a shared across the word boundary (cf. B, "syllable signs run across word boundaries").
7. L6 the old list had the barred 5 twice at the junction of zooms b and c (tile overlap): one token removed (331 -> 330).
8. L7 "[..7..] vigesimo [..]" -> "et[?] duplicata [..] vigesimo [..]": `ħ̄ ꝯo ẋ ŧ ∂ ħ- ꝏ` = [et] d-up-l-ic-at-a, the
   duplicate note as on f. 23 (f. 23 "duplicata die vigesimo quarto eiusdem"); the day after "vigesimo" (`2p` | L8 `bo`)
   is unread. So f. 102 is a duplicate, re-sent on the 2x of March, with f. 103 the clear letter of the original day.

**Where the cipher's wording differs from f. 103** (beyond the table above): "ve voglate intendere Re de Ragona" has no
"cum la M.tà del" (name sign only); "ve as[ħ̄] talmente" has one sign where f. 103 has "assecurarete" (the cipher seems
to abbreviate or drop the verb: unread, not forced); the end of L6 has 16 signs (`·‖· ħ ħ̄ ʒ̶ o## g‖ fa ♀ f 8 -o b p+ ƀ
÷ ɱ`, then L7 "trare") where f. 103 has "esso papa volesse sinistrar": no papa name sign and no spelled "papa" (∴ ft σ-)
in it, so the cipher's clause is worded differently (it ends -s-trare; "volese" may sit in `8 -o b p+ ƀ ÷`) and is left
unread; f. 103 has no duplicate note.

Still unread: L5 "as-[ħ̄]", the L6 15-sign clause, L7-8 the day of the duplicate.

## Counts

330 tokens: 310 read (26 [?]), 1 null, 19 unread = 94.2% of non-null tokens (worker E).
Before worker E: 331 tokens, 279 read, 1 null, 51 unread = 84.5%. Unread: L5 one sign ("as-[..]"), L6 the 16-sign clause before "-trare" the day of the duplicate (L7-8, 2 tokens).
Decryption

f102_reading.md

# BnF italien 1584 f. 102: cipher slip, Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, Milan 23 Mar 1447: reading

Illustris[?] fili noster carissime. Perché[?] nui intendemo pur el novo PAPA non inclinarse ad volere fare que[e][?] debe, dicemo che a nui pare, et cosi ve confortiamo et pregamo ve voglate intendere RE DE RAGONA al[qu]a[?]le havemo scripto el simile, e quando vedereti esso papa non volere fare quel debe, [che] desidera esso RE et VUY, intendandove la Maiest[?]ate soa et vui insieme ve as[..]talmente de li [f]acti soi et provediati e faciati con effecto inanci che esso papa abia piu firmato l[i] pedi, per modo et per manera che se ben [..16 signs..]-trare non posa. Data Mediolani vigesimo tertio marti(i), et[?] duplicata [..] vigesimo [..] [..]
Translation

f102_en.md

# BnF italien 1584 f. 102: cipher slip, Filippo Maria Visconti to Francesco Sforza, Milan 23 Mar 1447: English

Most dear son: since we understand that the new Pope is not inclined to do what he should, we advise and urge you to
come to an understanding with the King of Aragon, to whom we have written the same; and when you see that the Pope will
not do what he should and what the King and you desire, you and His Majesty together should make sure of his affairs
and act effectively before the Pope has set his feet more firmly, so that even if he wished to do harm he could not.
Milan, 23 March [1447].

Key: key.md

07 Sources

  • Bibliothèque nationale de France, italien 1584, on Gallica; italien 1583 ff. 110–158 on DECODE, R7902, R7904, R7907, R7914, R7915.

  • No edition or earlier decipherment of these slips or of the key was found (web search, October 2026). L. Cerioni, La diplomazia sforzesca nella seconda metà del Quattrocento e i suoi cifrari segreti (1970), covers the Sforza keys after 1450.
